\part{Foundations of Necessity and the Residuum Calculus}

\chapter{Foundations of Categorical Reverse Mathematics}

\label{ch:foundations}

\section{From Syntactic Axioms to Geometric Universes}

For half a century, the foundational program of Reverse Mathematics has sought to determine the exact logical strength of mathematical theorems. In the classical tradition inaugurated by Harvey Friedman and Stephen Simpson~\cite{Friedman75,Simpson09}, one fixes a single weak base theory---typically $\mathrm{RCA}_0$, a subsystem of second-order arithmetic equipped with recursive comprehension, $\Sigma^0_1$ induction, and the classical Law of the Excluded Middle ($\mathrm{LEM}$)---and calibrates a theorem $T$ by identifying the weakest set-existence axiom $A$ such that $\mathrm{RCA}_0 \vdash A \leftrightarrow T$. Hundreds of theorems across classical analysis, algebra, and combinatorics cluster into a linearly ordered hierarchy of five canonical subsystems, known as the ``Big Five'': $\mathrm{RCA}_0$, $\mathrm{WKL}_0$, $\mathrm{ACA}_0$, $\mathrm{ATR}_0$, and $\Pi^1_1\text{-}\mathrm{CA}_0$.

Constructive Reverse Mathematics, developed by Hajime Ishihara and his collaborators~\cite{Ishihara06}, refines this calibration by lowering the base theory from $\mathrm{RCA}_0$ to Errett Bishop's informal constructive mathematics ($\mathrm{BISH}$)~\cite{Bishop67}. Working without $\mathrm{LEM}$ or the Axiom of Choice ($\mathrm{AC}$), the constructive program reveals that many classical equivalences factor into a constructive analytical core combined with semi-classical logical principles---such as the Weak Law of Excluded Middle ($\mathrm{WLEM}$) or the Lesser Limited Principle of Omniscience ($\mathrm{LLPO}$)---or weak choice axioms like Countable Choice ($\mathrm{CC}$) and Dependent Choice ($\mathrm{DC}$).

Yet both classical and constructive reverse mathematics share a fundamental limitation: they treat the ambient mathematical universe as a fixed syntactic background and project all mathematical phenomena onto a one-dimensional ladder of formal axioms. Modern mathematics, however, does not live in a single formal language of second-order arithmetic. Since Alexander Grothendieck's work in algebraic geometry and F.~William Lawvere and Myles Tierney's axiomatization of elementary topoi~\cite{MacLaneMoerdijk92,Johnstone02}, and subsequently Jacob Lurie's synthesis of higher topos theory~\cite{Lurie09}, mathematicians have understood that mathematical structures are interpreted across a vast landscape of variable categorical universes and doctrines---sheaf topoi over topological spaces and locales, realizability topoi in computability theory, smooth topoi in synthetic differential geometry, commutative algebra with Frobenius lifts, and higher $(\infty, 1)$-topoi and $(\infty, \infty)$-categories in homotopy theory.

\textbf{Categorical Reverse Mathematics} ($\mathrm{CRM}$) shifts the calibration of mathematical theorems from syntactic axioms in a fixed formal system to the structural, algebraic, topological, and cohomological geometry of abstract mathematical doctrines. Rather than asking solely which set-existence axiom must be added to arithmetic to prove a theorem, $\mathrm{CRM}$ treats the ambient universe or algebraic doctrine $X \in \mathcal{D}_0$ as a variable space and asks: given two mathematical constructions $\Phi$ and $\Psi$ connected by a comparison transformation $\kappa : \Phi \Rightarrow \Psi$ that becomes an equivalence inside a stronger subdoctrine $\mathcal{D}_1 \subseteq \mathcal{D}_0$, what exact structural invariant $\mathrm{Res}(\kappa)(X)$ measures the gap separating $\mathcal{D}_0$ from $\mathcal{D}_1$?

\section{The Fundamental Equivalence: Doctrinal Fibrations, Comparison Squares, and the Universal Defect}\label{sec:fundamental-equivalence}

To establish Categorical Reverse Mathematics on a genuinely universal foundation---capable of treating Choice, constructive logic, the Axiom of Infinity, $p$-torsion algebra, and directed higher invertibility on equal footing without hard-coding specialized syntactic templates---we formulate the residuum operator via cloven \emph{doctrinal fibrations}, comparison data, and (specifically for adjunctions) Beck--Chevalley mate squares in the tradition of Lawvere~\cite{Lawvere69,Maietti05,Johnstone02} and Grothendieck's calculus of base change.

\begin{definition}[Doctrinal Comparisons, Adjoint Mate Squares, and $\mathrm{Dom}(\mathrm{Res})$]\label{def:comparison-triple}%
\lean{HomotopyCRM.DoctrinalFibration, HomotopyCRM.MateDoubleSquare, HomotopyCRM.mateDoubleSquare_mate_involutive}%
\leanok%
A \textbf{categorical doctrine} is a category, $2$-category, or $(\infty, 2)$-category $\mathcal{D}$ whose objects $X \in \mathcal{D}$ are mathematical universes or structured algebraic/higher-categorical objects equipped with a specified signature of categorical operations, limits, colimits, quantifiers, modalities, or coherent higher cells, and whose morphisms preserve that structure up to canonical isomorphism. Let $\mathbf{Doct}$ denote the meta-$2$-category (or $(\infty, 2)$-category) of categorical doctrines and structure-preserving functors.
\begin{enumerate}
  \item \textbf{Cloven Doctrinal Fibrations and Doctrinal Comparison Data:} A \textbf{cloven doctrinal fibration} is a Grothendieck fibration $p : \mathcal{E} \to \mathcal{B}$ (\texttt{DoctrinalFibration E B}) whose fibers $\mathcal{E}_B$ carry doctrinal structure preserved by Cartesian reindexing $u^* : \mathcal{E}_{B'} \to \mathcal{E}_B$ along $u : B \to B'$ (specializing via $\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{D}} : \mathcal{D} \to \mathcal{D}$ to any bare doctrine $\mathcal{D} \in \mathbf{Doct}$; an arbitrary fibration $p$ need \emph{not} admit left or right adjoints). A \textbf{doctrinal comparison datum} over a base doctrine $\mathcal{D}_0 \in \mathbf{Doct}$ and stronger subdoctrine $\mathcal{D}_1 \subseteq \mathcal{D}_0$ is a functor $F : \mathcal{A} \to \mathcal{B}$ or natural transformation $\kappa : \Phi \Rightarrow \Psi$ over $\mathcal{D}_0$ (optionally fibred over $\mathcal{E} \to \mathcal{B}$), whose fiber defects vanish precisely along $\mathcal{D}_1$.
  \item \textbf{Adjoint Mate Squares (Restricted Strictly to Adjunctions):} Whenever two horizontal \emph{adjunctions} $(L \dashv R : \mathcal{D} \rightleftarrows \mathcal{E})$ and $(L' \dashv R' : \mathcal{D}' \rightleftarrows \mathcal{E}')$ are given alongside vertical functors $u : \mathcal{D} \to \mathcal{D}'$ and $F : \mathcal{E} \to \mathcal{E}'$ (\texttt{MateDoubleSquare}), any $2$-cell $\alpha : L' \circ u \Longrightarrow F \circ L$ transposes canonically via the mate equivalence \texttt{mateEquiv} to its \textbf{Beck--Chevalley mate}
  \[
    \mu_\alpha \;:\; u \circ R \Longrightarrow R' \circ F
  \]
  (\texttt{beckChevalleyMate}), whose unit--counit transposition $\alpha \mapsto \mu_\alpha$ is strictly involutive (\texttt{mateDoubleSquare\_ mate\_ involutive}). Adjoint mate squares govern internal-versus-external base change (§\ref{sec:base-fiber} and Chapter~\ref{ch:base-fiber}) whenever $\gamma^* \dashv \gamma_*$ or $f^* \dashv \Pi_f$ is present.
  \item \textbf{Doctrinal Comparison Triples and $\mathrm{Dom}(\mathrm{Res})$:} Packaging $\mathcal{D}_0 \supseteq \mathcal{D}_1$ with $\kappa : \Phi \Rightarrow \Psi$ (including $\kappa := \mu_\alpha$ over $\mathcal{D}_1 := \mathrm{Loc}_{\mathrm{BC}}(\alpha)$ in an adjoint mate square, or the comparison unit/projection of a comparison functor $F : \mathcal{A} \to \mathcal{B}$) defines a \textbf{doctrinal comparison triple}
  \[
    \mathfrak{T} \;=\; \bigl(\mathcal{D}_0 \supseteq \mathcal{D}_1, \quad \kappa : \Phi \Longrightarrow \Psi\bigr),
  \]
  where $\kappa$ becomes an equivalence (or satisfies the specified fiber-splitting criterion) along $\mathcal{D}_1$. The \textbf{domain of the categorical residuum}, denoted $\mathrm{Dom}(\mathrm{Res})$, is the collection of all such comparison triples across mathematics.
\end{enumerate}
\end{definition}

\begin{definition}[Truncated Homotopy-Fiber Defect and Categorical Residuum]\label{def:residuum}%
\uses{def:comparison-triple}%
Let $\mathfrak{T} = (\mathcal{D}_0 \supseteq \mathcal{D}_1, \; \kappa : \Phi \Longrightarrow \Psi) \in \mathrm{Dom}(\mathrm{Res})$ be induced by a doctrinal comparison functor $F_X : \mathcal{A}(X) \to \mathcal{B}(X)$ (or an adjoint mate $\mu_{\alpha, X} : \Phi(X) \to \Psi(X)$) evaluated at $X \in \mathcal{D}_0$. At a target object or base test object $y \in \mathcal{B}(X)$ (e.g.\ a terminal base, a completion target, or a candidate base structure), the \textbf{mode-$n$ homotopy-fiber defect} ($n \in \{-1, 0, \infty\}$) of $F_X$ over $y$ is measured by its $n$-truncated homotopy fiber (iso-comma fiber)
\[
  \bigl\|\mathrm{hofib}_y(F_X)\bigr\|_n \;:=\; \bigl\|\mathcal{A}(X) \times^h_{\mathcal{B}(X)} \{y\}\bigr\|_n,
\]
which is trivial precisely when $\|\mathrm{hofib}_y(F_X)\|_n \simeq *$: at $n = -1$ (inhabitation defect) it detects emptiness of the fiber without requiring contractibility; at $n = 0$ (multiplicity/orbit defect) it extracts the isomorphism set $\pi_0(\mathrm{hofib}_y(F_X))$; and at $n = \infty$ across all $y$ it detects failure of $F_X$ to be an equivalence ($\mathrm{hofib}_y(F_X) \simeq *$).

A \textbf{categorical residuum} for $\mathfrak{T}$ is an admissible structural invariant $\mathcal{R} = \mathrm{Res}(\kappa)$ on $X \in \mathcal{D}_0$ characterizing the vanishing locus $\mathcal{D}_1$ of the relevant homotopy-fiber defects:
\[
  X \in \mathcal{D}_0 \quad \text{and} \quad \mathcal{R}(X) \iff X \in \mathcal{D}_1,
\]
written compactly as $\mathcal{D}_0 + \mathrm{Res}(\kappa) \iff \mathcal{D}_1$ (the \textbf{Fundamental Equivalence of Categorical Reverse Mathematics}). In particular, in the topos-logical specialization $\mathcal{D}_0 = \mathbf{Mod}(A) := \{\mathcal{E} \in \mathbf{Topos} \mid \mathcal{E} \models A\}$ and $\mathcal{D}_1 = \mathbf{Mod}(B) \subsetneq \mathbf{Mod}(A)$ for internal principles $B \implies A$, $\mathcal{R} = (A \Rightarrow_{\mathbf{Topos}} B)$ validates $\mathbf{Mod}(A) \cap \mathbf{Mod}(\mathcal{R}) = \mathbf{Mod}(B)$, or $A + \mathcal{R}(\mathcal{E}) \iff B$.
\end{definition}

In the topos specialization, the categorical residuum $(A \Rightarrow_{\mathbf{Topos}} B)$ acts order-theoretically as a relative pseudocomplement in the lattice of universe classes (Figure~\ref{fig:comparison-triple-residuum}(c)): a strong theorem $B$ factors orthogonally into its internal deductive core $A$ together with the ambient geometry $\mathcal{R}(\mathcal{E})$ required of the surrounding world. Crucially, Categorical Reverse Mathematics must be distinguished from Olivia Caramello's program of ``toposes as bridges'' via Morita equivalence~\cite{Caramello18}. Caramello's methodology compares two \emph{different syntactic geometric theories} $T_1$ and $T_2$ sharing the \emph{exact same classifying topos} $\mathcal{E}[T_1] \simeq \mathcal{E}[T_2]$, transferring properties across alternative sites $(\mathbb{C}_{T_1}, J_1)$ and $(\mathbb{C}_{T_2}, J_2)$ of a single invariant space. By contrast, $\mathrm{CRM}$ evaluates homotopy-fiber residua across doctrines $\mathcal{D}_0 \supsetneq \mathcal{D}_1$ of \emph{genuinely unequal foundational strength}.

\begin{proposition}[Three-Mode Classification of Homotopy-Fiber Obstructions]\label{prop:tri-modal-factorization}%
\lean{HomotopyCRM.intToZMod2TypeMode_sectionSpace_nontrivial, HomotopyCRM.not_intToZMod2TypeMode_contractible, HomotopyCRM.isEmpty_intToZMod2AbMode_sectionSpace, HomotopyCRM.profiniteLassoTowerMode_hasPhantom, HomotopyCRM.stepProductTowerMode_hasPhantom}%
\uses{def:comparison-triple,def:residuum}%
Doctrinal comparison data across $\mathrm{Dom}(\mathrm{Res})$ evaluate canonically along three fundamental homotopy-fiber defect modes (Figure~\ref{fig:comparison-triple-residuum}(b); note that an invertible morphism between cloven fibrations admits the standard two-factor vertical + Cartesian factorization, while $\mathcal{J}$-completion defects arise whenever a specified completion functor $\eta : \mathcal{E} \to \widehat{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathcal{J}}$ is fixed):
\begin{enumerate}
  \item \textbf{Section-Splitting Obstruction ($\mathrm{Res}_{\mathrm{sec}}$, Internal vs.\ External Polarity):} Evaluating $(-1)$-connected section inhabitation (\texttt{SectionObstructionMode}) along dependent-product or global-section functors separates two genuinely distinct polarities:
  \begin{itemize}
    \item \emph{Internal slice splitting:} $\|\Pi_f(p)\|_{-1} \simeq 1_{\mathcal{E}/Y}$, which characterizes $\mathrm{IAC}$ and already holds on $\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{odd}} \twoheadrightarrow 1_{\mathbf{Nom}}$ in $\mathbf{Nom}$ and $\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z} \twoheadrightarrow 1$ in $\mathbf{Set}^{\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}}$.
    \item \emph{External global-section splitting:} $\|\Gamma(\Pi_f(p))\|_{-1} \simeq 1_{\mathbf{Set}}$ (for $Y = 1_{\mathcal{E}}$, $\|\Gamma(E)\|_{-1} \simeq *$), which characterizes $\mathrm{EAC}$ and detects $\Gamma(\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{odd}}) = \emptyset$ and $\Gamma(\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}) = \emptyset$.
  \end{itemize}
  Furthermore, $(-1)$-connected inhabitation is strictly weaker than $(-2)$-contractibility: for $q : \mathbb{Z} \twoheadrightarrow \mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}$, the set-level section space is non-trivial (\texttt{intToZMod2TypeMode\_ sectionSpace\_ nontrivial}) and therefore inhabited yet not contractible (\texttt{not\_ intToZMod2TypeMode\_ contractible}), whereas its abelian group section fiber is empty (\texttt{isEmpty\_ intToZMod2AbMode\_ sectionSpace}).
  \item \textbf{Specified $\mathcal{J}$-Completion / Tower Phantom ($\mathrm{Res}_{\mathrm{lim}}$):} Relative to a specified $\mathcal{J}$-completion unit $\eta : \mathcal{E} \to \widehat{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathcal{J}}$ (such as $\mathbf{FinSet} \hookrightarrow \mathrm{Pro}(\mathbf{FinSet})$ or Postnikov limit completion $\mathcal{C} \to RL(\mathcal{C})$), \emph{phantoms} are objects $X_\bullet \in \widehat{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathcal{J}}$ whose homotopy fiber $\mathrm{hofib}_{X_\bullet}(\eta)$ is empty (\texttt{TowerPhantomMode}), alongside derived $\varprojlim^1$ obstructions---evaluated in Lean on $-1 \in \widehat{\mathbb{Z}} \setminus \mathrm{range}(\mathbb{N})$ inside $\widehat{\mathbb{N}}_{\mathrm{lasso}}$ (\texttt{profiniteLassoTowerMode\_ hasPhantom}) and on $X_\bullet \in RL(\mathcal{Q}) \setminus \mathcal{Q}$ (\texttt{stepProductTowerMode\_ hasPhantom}).
  \item \textbf{Forgetful Homotopy-Fiber Lift and Orbit Quotient ($\mathrm{Res}_{\mathrm{lift}}$):} Evaluating a forgetful doctrinal functor $U : \mathcal{E}' \to \mathcal{E}$ directly at a \emph{base} object $B \in \mathcal{E}$ via its iso-comma homotopy fiber over the underlying groupoid/core $U^{\simeq}$,
  \[
    \mathrm{hofib}_B(U^{\simeq}) \;:=\; \sum_{E' \in (\mathcal{E}')^{\simeq}} \mathrm{Equiv}_{\mathcal{E}}\bigl(U(E'), B\bigr)
  \]
  (\texttt{ForgetfulLiftMode}), separates \emph{$B$-rigidified lifts} from \emph{lift isomorphism classes}: $\pi_0(\mathrm{hofib}_B(U^{\simeq}))$ classifies pairs $(E', \theta : U(E') \xrightarrow{\sim} B)$ (retaining the conjugation action of $\mathrm{Aut}_{\mathcal{E}}(B)$), whereas isomorphism classes of $\mathcal{E}'$-lifts of $B$ are classified by the \textbf{orbit quotient} $\pi_0(\mathrm{hofib}_B(U^{\simeq})) / \mathrm{Aut}_{\mathcal{E}}(B)$, and higher homotopy groups $\pi_k(\mathrm{hofib}_B(U^{\simeq}))$ ($k \ge 1$) carry residual loop-automorphism symmetries such as $\Omega(\nu\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{Anima}}, [q,q]) \cong \mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Because every comparison datum evaluates the gap between $\mathcal{A}$ and $\mathcal{B}$ at a base or target object $y \in \mathcal{B}$, the three modes reflect the three fundamental evaluation targets:
\begin{itemize}
  \item For \textbf{Mode~(i)}, evaluating section inhabitation internally via $\|\Pi_f(p)\|_{-1}$ tests $\mathrm{IAC}$ (satisfied in every atomic permutation topos and finite group presheaf topos $\mathbf{Set}^{\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}}$), whereas applying $\Gamma = \mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{E}}(1_{\mathcal{E}}, -)$ before truncating tests $\|\Gamma(\Pi_f(p))\|_{-1}$ ($\mathrm{EAC}$), exposing $\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{odd}}$ and $\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}$; explicitly on $q : \mathbb{Z} \twoheadrightarrow \mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}$, $\{0, 3\}^{\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}}$ gives distinct set sections $s(0)=0, s(1)\in\{1, 3\}$, whereas $2\cdot s(1) = s(0) = 0$ forbids abelian-group sections in $\mathbb{Z}$.
  \item For \textbf{Mode~(ii)}, an element $X_\bullet \in \widehat{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathcal{J}}$ fails to lie in the essential image of $\eta : \mathcal{E} \to \widehat{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathcal{J}}$ precisely when $\mathrm{hofib}_{X_\bullet}(\eta) = \emptyset$; evaluating the pro-system $L_{p,q}$ and Postnikov prefix chain $(e^{(n-1)})_{n \ge 1}$ exhibits $-1 \in \widehat{\mathbb{Z}}$ and $X_\bullet \in RL(\mathcal{Q}) \setminus \mathcal{Q}$ as concrete empty-fiber phantoms.
  \item For \textbf{Mode~(iii)}, replacing $(E', \theta : U(E') \xrightarrow{\sim} B)$ by an isomorphic pair $(E'', \theta' : U(E'') \xrightarrow{\sim} B)$ along an $\mathcal{E}'$-isomorphism $\alpha : E' \xrightarrow{\sim} E''$ forces $\theta' = \theta \circ U(\alpha)^{-1}$ (rigidified path-components $\pi_0\,\mathrm{hofib}_B(U^{\simeq})$), while varying only the forgetful identification $\theta$ up to $g \in \mathrm{Aut}_{\mathcal{E}}(B)$ yields the $\mathrm{Aut}_{\mathcal{E}}(B)$-conjugation action, so isomorphism classes of lifts in $\mathcal{E}'$ correspond bijectively to the orbit quotient $\pi_0\,\mathrm{hofib}_B(U^{\simeq}) / \mathrm{Aut}_{\mathcal{E}}(B)$ (giving $\pi_0(\mathrm{hofib}_*(1 \to \mathbf{B}G)) \cong G$, $1$ isomorphism class $G/\mathrm{Aut}(*)$ on $\mathbf{B}G$ with $\pi_1 \cong G$, and $16$ rigidified lifts carving into $6$ $\mathrm{GL}_2(\mathbb{F}_2)$-orbits on $(R_{2,2}, \phi_{\mathrm{aug}})$ in Proposition~\ref{prop:delta-three-stage-tower}).\qedhere
\end{itemize}
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Framed Equipments, Axiom Encoding, and Morita Invariance]\label{rem:framed-equipments}%
\uses{def:comparison-triple,def:residuum,prop:tri-modal-factorization}%
Organizing cloven doctrinal fibrations globally yields a \emph{framed bicategory}---or more generally a framed $(\infty, 2)$-equipment $\mathbb{D}$ in the sense of Shulman~\cite{Shulman08}---governed by the higher calculus of mates for two-variable fibrations established by Haugseng, Hebestreit, Linskens, and Nuiten~\cite{HHLN23}. Leading our exposition with concrete comparison data and adjoint mate squares rather than axiomatic equipments keeps the foundation directly computable, while separating two logically distinct steps: first, the \emph{modeling choice} that encodes an informal principle $\Phi$ into an explicit comparison datum $\alpha_\Phi$ (for instance, choosing which covers test Choice or which completion tests infinity); and second, the \emph{Morita-invariant residuum} $\mathrm{Res}(\kappa_\Phi)$, which depends solely on the equivalence class of the comparison inside $\mathbb{D}$.
\end{remark}

\begin{center}\emph{[Figure omitted in blueprint edition; see Royal Octavo monograph PDF.]}\label{fig:comparison-triple-residuum}\end{center}

Placing general comparison triples and their homotopy-fiber defects on equal footing organizes Categorical Reverse Mathematics along \textbf{Five Foundational Coordinate Axes}:

\begin{enumerate}
  \item \textbf{Choice / Splitting ($\mathrm{EAC}, \mathrm{IAC}, \mathrm{IDC}_\omega$):} Mode~(i) horizontal base change over $\mathcal{D}_0 = \mathbf{Topos}$ ($\mathbf{GTop}$) vs.\ $\mathcal{D}_1 = \mathbf{Topos}_{\mathrm{AC}}$, separating internal slice connectedness $\|\Pi_f(p)\|_{-1} \simeq 1_{\mathcal{E}/Y}$ ($\mathrm{IAC}$) from external global-section connectedness $\|\Gamma(\Pi_f(p))\|_{-1} \simeq *$ ($\mathrm{EAC}$) on covers $E \twoheadrightarrow B$ and $\mathrm{im}(\mathrm{InvStr}) \hookrightarrow E_{\mathrm{rel}}$. Residua include non-abelian torsors $H^1(\mathcal{E}/Y; G)$ ($\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{odd}}$ in $\mathbf{Nom}$), definable $\varprojlim^1_{\mathrm{def}}$, and the \texttt{ICaTT} witness-stream gap (Chapter~\ref{ch:realizability-sites}, Theorem~\ref{thm:coinductive-stream-choice}).
  \item \textbf{Constructive Logic ($\mathrm{LEM}, \mathrm{WLEM}$):} Modes~(i) and~(ii) across $\mathcal{D}_0 = \mathbf{HeytingCat} \supseteq \mathbf{BooleanCat} = \mathcal{D}_1$, comparing classical truth values to $\Omega$ via $1+1 \hookrightarrow \Omega$ and $\mathrm{Sub}_{\mathrm{dec}}(X) \hookrightarrow \mathrm{Sub}(X)$. Residua isolate boundary sheaves $\partial X$, $\neg\neg$-dense nuclei, and Dedekind--MacNeille isomorphism failure.
  \item \textbf{Arithmetic Infinity ($\mathrm{NNO}$, $\mu F$ vs.\ $\nu F$):} Splitting cleanly into Mode~(ii) pro-initial completion $\widehat{\mathbb{N}}_{\mathrm{lasso}} \notin \mathbf{FinSet}$ (trivialized by adjoining an $\mathrm{NNO}$ in $\mathbf{Pretopos}_{\mathrm{NNO}}$) and finitary/well-founded stabilization $\mathrm{Res}_{\mathrm{Ded}}(X),\, \prod_{\mathbb{N}} A / \bigoplus_{\mathbb{N}} A$ (trivialized by $\neg\mathsf{Inf}$ in $\mathbf{FinSet}$), together with step-product phantoms $X_\bullet \in RL(\mathcal{Q}) \setminus \mathcal{Q}$ (Theorem~\ref{thm:step-product-counterexample}).
  \item \textbf{Characteristic / $p$-Torsion:} Mode~(iii) vertical forgetful fiber along $U : \delta\text{-}\mathbf{Ring}_p \to \mathbf{FrobLiftRing}_p$ over base Frobenius-lift rings $(R, \phi)$ via Joyal's~\cite{Joyal85} identity $\phi(x) = x^p + p\,\delta(x)$, governed over $p$-torsion bases by a two-stage lift tower in $\mathrm{hofib}_{(R,\phi)}(U^{\simeq})$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:delta-three-stage-tower}), paired with Mode~(ii) colimit completion $A \to A_{\mathrm{perf}}$ vs.\ the Nygaard filtration separating relative perfection from general prisms.
  \item \textbf{Directional Invertibility ($\pi_{\le 0}$):} Mode~(ii) completion $\mathcal{C} \hookrightarrow RL(\mathcal{C})$ across $\mathrm{Cat}^R_{(\infty,\infty)}$ alongside Mode~(i)/(iii) comparison $\mathrm{id} \Rightarrow \pi_{\le 0}$ comparing directed stabilization $\Omega_{\mathrm{dir}}$ to groupoidification. Residua recover stable homotopy stems $\pi_*^s(\mathbb{S}_{\mathrm{top}})$ from $\mathbf{B}^\infty(\mathbf{FinSet}^{\simeq})$, step-product phantoms ($\mathcal{Q}$), and directed facet cycles $F_n$ ($n \ge 3$, Theorem~\ref{thm:directed-cubical-amalgamation}).
\end{enumerate}

\begin{remark}[Grounding the Five Axes in Five Anchor Witnesses]\label{rem:anchor-witnesses}
To prevent the residuum calculus from remaining abstract formalism, every coordinate axis in Categorical Reverse Mathematics is calibrated against five concrete, self-contained \emph{anchor witnesses} previewed in the Preface:
\begin{itemize}
  \item \textbf{The Parity Torsor $\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{odd}}$ (Axes I \& II):} Inside Schanuel's nominal topos $\mathbf{Nom} = \mathbf{Cont}(\mathrm{Sym}(\mathbb{N}))$, the set of odd-sized finite subsets of $\mathbb{N}$ has no global invariant element ($\Gamma(\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{odd}}) = \emptyset$), yet splits locally as soon as any single atom $a \in \mathbb{N}$ is named. It represents a non-zero cohomology class $[\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{odd}}] = \partial(1) \neq 0 \in H^1(\mathbf{Nom}; \mathrm{EvenFinset})$, witnessing the exact obstruction separating $\mathrm{IAC}$ from $\mathrm{AC}$ in a Boolean, two-valued universe despite pro-discrete blindness ($H^1(\mathbf{Nom}; \mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}) = 0$).
  \item \textbf{The $2$-Torsion Ring $R_2 = \mathbb{Z}[\eta]/(2\eta, \eta^2)$ (Axis IV):} In the doctrine of $\delta$-rings at $p = 2$, $R_2$ is isomorphic as an additive abelian group to $\mathbb{Z} \oplus \mathbb{F}_2$ (though \emph{not} as commutative rings to $\mathbb{Z} \times \mathbb{F}_2$, because $\eta \neq 0$ while $\eta^2 = 0$), and $2\eta = 0$ blocks naive division by $2$. Joyal's binomial identity $\delta(x + y) = \delta(x) + \delta(y) - xy$ reveals two distinct $\delta$-structures $\delta_0(\eta) = 0$ and $\delta_1(\eta) = \eta$ lifting the \textbf{augmentation Frobenius endomorphism} $\phi(a + b\eta) = a$ (where $\phi(\eta) = 0$), proving that $\mathrm{hofib}_{(R,\phi)}(U)$ over $p$-torsion rings is a non-trivial $\mathrm{Der}_\phi$-torsor, while the torsion-free dual-integer ring \texttt{DualInt} ($\mathbb{Z}[\varepsilon]/(\varepsilon^2)$) witnesses the collapse of the Nygaard filtration under relative colimit perfection.
  \item \textbf{The Step-Product $\mathcal{Q}$ on $[1] = \{0 < 1\}$ (Axes III \& V):} In weak $(\infty, \infty)$-categories, build the finite gaunt $k$-categories $\mathcal{A}_k = \mathcal{M}_{k+1}$ with two objects $\{0_k, 1_k\}$ such that $\mathrm{Hom}(0_k, 0_k) \simeq \mathrm{Hom}(0_k, 1_k) \simeq \mathrm{Hom}(1_k, 0_k) \simeq *$ whereas the diagonal hom-object $\mathrm{Hom}(1_k, 1_k) = \mathcal{M}_k \not\simeq *$ carries non-trivial $k$-dimensional structure. Inside $\prod_{k \ge 1} \mathcal{A}_k$, the restricted-tail step-product $\mathcal{Q}$ spanned by finite-prefix indicator sequences $e^{(m)} = (1_1, \dots, 1_m, 0_{m+1}, \dots)$ satisfies $\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{Q}}(e^{(m)}, e^{(r)}) \simeq \prod_{k=1}^{\min(m, r)} \mathcal{M}_k$ of rank $\min(m, r)$, yet its $n$-truncations $y_n := \ell_n(e^{(n-1)}) \in \pi_{\le n}(\mathcal{Q})$ assemble into a pure Postnikov phantom $X_\bullet \in RL(\mathcal{Q}) \setminus \mathcal{Q}$, separating $\mathrm{Cat}^\omega_{(\infty,\infty)}$ from $\mathrm{Cat}^L_{(\infty,\infty)}$ without topological spheres (Theorem~\ref{thm:step-product-counterexample}).
  \item \textbf{The Facet-Supported Directed Triangle $F_3 = \partial\Delta^2$ (Axis V \& Site Geometry):} In directed combinatorial geometry, gluing two acyclic directed complexes along a flow-convex overlap preserves acyclicity ($n = 2$), whereas gluing around the boundary of $F_3 = \partial\Delta^2$ or a directed $n$-cube fails for all $n \ge 3$ by closing an irreversible directed cycle (Theorem~\ref{thm:directed-cubical-amalgamation}).
  \item \textbf{The Cantor Smooth-End Quotient $\mathcal{Q}_{E_0} = 2^{\mathbb{N}}/E_0$ and $\mathrm{UHF}(2^\infty)$ (Axes I, III, \& V):} In light condensed sets $\mathbf{Sh}(\mathrm{Prof}_{\aleph_0})$, the Vitali/Hamel tail quotient $\mathcal{Q}_{E_0} = \prod_{\mathbb{N}} \mathbb{F}_2 / \bigoplus_{\mathbb{N}} \mathbb{F}_2$ sits suspended between Freedman's topological collapse ($*$), discontinuous $\mathsf{ZFC}$ splitting ($\mathbb{R}$), and trivial Polish reflection ($\pi_0^{\mathrm{Pol}}(\mathcal{Q}_{E_0}) \simeq 1$), precipitating a non-splitting condensed class $[q] \in \mathrm{Ext}^1_{\mathrm{CondAb}}(\mathcal{Q}_{E_0}, \bigoplus_{\mathbb{N}}\mathbb{F}_2)$ alongside the Connes noncommutative $C^*$-modulus $C^*(\mathcal{G}_{E_0}) \cong \mathrm{UHF}(2^\infty)$ ($K_0 \cong \mathbb{Z}[\tfrac{1}{2}]$) and its Pauli-$Z$ $E_0^{\mathrm{even}}$ parity grading (Theorem~\ref{thm:double-solvent-r4} and Section~\ref{subsec:uhf-cstar-e0}).\qedhere
\end{itemize}
\end{remark}

\section{The Admissibility Problem, Doctrinal Philosophies, and Orthogonal Invariants}\label{sec:admissibility}

At the heart of Categorical Reverse Mathematics lies a subtle methodological hazard that we call the \emph{Triviality Trap}. If we place no restrictions on what constitutes a valid condition $\mathcal{R}(\mathcal{E})$, one could simply define $\mathcal{R}(\mathcal{E})$ to be the internal material implication $A \to B$ (or the external implication $(\mathcal{E} \models A) \implies (\mathcal{E} \models B)$), or set $\mathrm{Def}(\alpha)(X) := \kappa_X$ literally without structural reduction. Syntactically, $A \wedge (A \to B) \leftrightarrow B$ is a tautology, but it provides zero structural insight into why $B$ fails in general models of $A$.

To prevent tautological restatements, $\mathrm{CRM}$ enforces an \emph{Admissibility Criterion}: the residuum must be formulated purely in terms of \emph{orthogonal structural invariants} of the ambient doctrine $X \in \mathcal{D}_0$ rather than the specific sentences or instances appearing in $\mathcal{D}_1$. In a general elementary or Grothendieck topos $\mathcal{E}$, admissible invariants fall into four foundational families (supplemented on Axes~III--V by initial-algebra/tail quotients, $\delta$-torsor and Nygaard invariants, and Postnikov/amalgamation obstructions):

\begin{itemize}

  \item \textbf{Subobject Classifier Geometry:} Order-theoretic and algebraic properties of the truth-value object $\Omega$, such as Booleanness ($\Omega \simeq 1+1$), Stone/De Morgan structure ($\neg\phi \vee \neg\neg\phi = \top$), G\"odel--Dummett linearity, or internal compactness of $\Omega$.

  \item \textbf{Generator and Exactness Conditions:} Projectivity and generating properties of canonical objects, such as whether the terminal object $1_{\mathcal{E}}$ is \emph{externally projective} ($\Gamma(X) \neq \emptyset$ for every epimorphism $X \twoheadrightarrow 1_{\mathcal{E}}$), whether $\mathcal{E}$ is \emph{two-valued} ($\mathrm{Sub}_{\mathcal{E}}(1_{\mathcal{E}}) \simeq \{0, 1\}$) or \emph{well-pointed} (two-valued with $1_{\mathcal{E}}$ an externally projective generator), whether subobjects of $1_{\mathcal{E}}$ generate $\mathcal{E}$ (localicity), or whether $\mathcal{E}$ possesses enough projectives ($\mathrm{COSHEP}$). Crucially, two-valuedness does \emph{not} imply external projectivity of $1_{\mathcal{E}}$: in any atomic permutation topos $\mathbf{Cont}(\mathcal{G})$ possessing a proper open subgroup $U \subsetneq \mathcal{G}$ (such as Schanuel's nominal topos $\mathbf{Nom}$), $\mathrm{Sub}(1_{\mathcal{E}}) = \{0, 1\}$, yet the canonical coset epimorphism $\mathcal{G}/U \twoheadrightarrow 1_{\mathcal{E}}$ has empty global sections ($\Gamma(\mathcal{G}/U) = (\mathcal{G}/U)^{\mathcal{G}} = \emptyset$).

  \item \textbf{Site and Locale Topology:} For a Grothendieck sheaf topos $\mathcal{E} \simeq \mathbf{Sh}(\mathbb{C}, J)$, topological invariants of the underlying site or locale, including localicity, coherence (compactness of coverings), zero-dimensionality (refinement by clopen partitions), and the countable chain condition ($\omega$-presentability).

  \item \textbf{Cohomological Vanishing Obstructions:} Homological and sheaf-cohomological invariants, such as the vanishing of $\mathrm{Ext}^1_{\mathcal{E}}$ or first sheaf cohomology $H^1(\mathcal{E}, G) = 0$ for canonical classes of internal structure groups $G$, diagnosing when locally split structures glue into global sections.

\end{itemize}

Rather than leaving admissibility informal, we internalize the interaction between universes and invariant probes as a precise mathematical structure inspired by Shulman's~\cite{Shulman20} $2$-Chu-Dialectica pairing.

\begin{definition}[Doctrinal Philosophy, Pareto Frontier, and Epistemic Regimes]\label{def:doctrinal-philosophy}%
\uses{def:comparison-triple,def:residuum,rem:framed-equipments}%
A \textbf{(doctrinal) philosophy} is a triple $\mathfrak{P} = (\mathbb{D}, \mathcal{P}, \langle -, - \rangle)$ (\texttt{DoctrinalPhilosophy D P}) consisting of a locally small, fibrant framed doctrinal equipment $\mathbb{D}$~\cite{Shulman08,HHLN23}, a stratified suite $\mathcal{P}$ of structural probes, and a pairing $\langle X, P \rangle \in \mathcal{V}$ ($X \models P$). The induced Galois connection
\[
  \begin{aligned}
    \mathrm{Th}_{\mathcal{P}}(\mathcal{U}) &:=\; \{P \in \mathcal{P} \mid \forall X \in \mathcal{U},\; X \models P\}, \\
    \mathrm{Ext}_{\mathcal{P}}(Q) &:=\; \{X \in \mathbb{D} \mid \forall P \in Q,\; X \models P\}
  \end{aligned}
\]
generates a Moore closure $\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{P}) := \mathrm{Ext}_{\mathcal{P}}(\mathrm{Th}_{\mathcal{P}}(-))$ on subsets of $\mathbb{D}$ (\texttt{mooreClosureOperator}). Given a hypothesis class $H \subseteq \mathbb{D}$ (\texttt{hyp}) and conclusion class $C \subseteq \mathbb{D}$ (\texttt{concl}) with $H \not\subseteq C$ (such as $H = \mathbf{Mod}(\mathrm{IAC})$ and $C = \mathbf{Mod}(\mathrm{AC})$), let $T := (\mathbb{D} \setminus H) \cup C$ denote the \textbf{material-implication target} ($T = \mathbf{Mod}(A \to B)$, so $H \cap E \subseteq C$ holds iff $E \subseteq T$). The \textbf{Pareto Residuum Frontier} $\mathrm{Res}_{\mathcal{P}}^{\min}(H \Rightarrow C)$ consists of all inclusion-maximal (deductively weakest) $\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{P})$-closed extents $E$ satisfying $H \cap E \subseteq C$ (equivalently $E \subseteq T$). A philosophy $\mathfrak{P}$ natively calibrates five epistemic regimes:
\begin{enumerate}
  \item \textbf{Dogma (Triviality Trap):} The implication target $T = (\mathbb{D} \setminus H) \cup C$ is itself $\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{P})$-closed ($\mathrm{Cl}_{\mathcal{P}}(T) = T$, e.g.\ when $A \to B$ is syntactically encoded in $\mathcal{P}$ without weaker witness separation), collapsing $\mathrm{Res}_{\mathcal{P}}^{\min}(H \Rightarrow C) = \{T\}$.
  \item \textbf{Blindness:} $\mathrm{Res}_{\mathcal{P}}^{\min}(H \Rightarrow C) = \emptyset$ (or, relative to a non-vacuity core $K \subseteq H \cap C$, $\mathrm{Cl}_{\mathcal{P}}(K) \not\subseteq T$) despite non-trivial defects (for instance, pro-discrete finite cohomology $H^1(-;\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z})$ is blind to $\mathcal{T}_{\mathrm{odd}}$ in $\mathbf{Nom}$, and Polish $\pi_0^{\mathrm{Pol}}$ is blind to $\mathcal{Q}_{E_0}$ and $\mathrm{UHF}(2^\infty)$).
  \item \textbf{Schism:} $|\mathrm{Res}_{\mathcal{P}}^{\min}(H \Rightarrow C)| \ge 2$ forms a genuine antichain of incomparable geometric trade-offs inside $T$.
  \item \textbf{Heresy:} A concrete witness $W \in E_1 \setminus E_2$ separating two candidate residua $E_1, E_2$.
  \item \textbf{Conversions and Bi-interpretation:} A mixed-variance Chu morphism~\cite{Shulman20} $(F, G) : \mathfrak{P}_1 \to \mathfrak{P}_2$ with adjoint pairing $\langle F(X), P_2 \rangle \simeq \langle X, G(P_2) \rangle$; $(F, G)$ is a \textbf{bi-interpretation} when its induced closure maps identify $\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{P}_1) \simeq \mathcal{M}(\mathcal{P}_2)$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{definition}

Could negative stability rules alone (such as closure under binary products, open subtopoi, or Lawvere--Tierney localizations) filter out Dogma without specifying an explicit probe suite $\mathcal{P}$? The following proposition settles that question negatively while establishing an exact finite Pareto Schism inside Choice Geometry.

\begin{proposition}[Inquisitor's Impossibility and Finite Pareto Schism]\label{prop:anti-casuistry-schism}%
\lean{HomotopyCRM.DoctrinalPhilosophy, HomotopyCRM.inquisitor_impossibility_casuistry, HomotopyCRM.monoid_proj1_iff_rightZero, HomotopyCRM.m1_projSub_and_not_localic, HomotopyCRM.vPoset_localic_not_proj1, HomotopyCRM.diamond_proj1_not_projSub, HomotopyCRM.not_catPresheafIAC_m1Cat, HomotopyCRM.not_catPresheafIAC_vPosetOpCat, HomotopyCRM.twoGroupCat_iac_and_not_ac, HomotopyCRM.freydCover_dogma_banishment, HomotopyCRM.choiceConcl_ne_extProj, HomotopyCRM.pareto_incomparable_projSub_localic, HomotopyCRM.M1SliceLTT.m1_slice_lt_count}%
\leanok%
\uses{def:doctrinal-philosophy}%
Let $\mathfrak{P} = (\mathbb{D}, \mathcal{P}, \langle -, - \rangle)$ be a doctrinal philosophy on a product-complete meet-semilattice of doctrines.
\begin{enumerate}
  \item \textbf{Inquisitor's Impossibility (Anti-Casuistry Lemma):} For any monoid $\Sigma$ of product-preserving doctrinal endomorphisms (\texttt{ProductEndo}) and any product-closed target upper-bound class $T \subseteq \mathbb{D}$ (such as $T = \mathbf{Mod}(A \to B)$ when product-closed), its modal $S4$-interior $\Box_\Sigma(T) := \{X \in \mathbb{D} \mid \forall \sigma \in \Sigma,\; \sigma(X) \in T\}$ is itself product-closed, $\Sigma$-invariant, contained in $T$, and uniquely maximal among all $\Sigma$-closed subclasses $\mathcal{U} \subseteq T$ (\texttt{inquisitor\_ impossibility\_ casuistry}). Hence negative stability laws alone cannot banish Dogma without a kataphatic probe suite $\mathcal{P}$.
  \item \textbf{Open-Subtopos Stability and Supports Split ($\mathrm{SS}$):} On presheaves $\mathbf{Set}^M$ over a monoid $M$, external projectivity of $1$ ($\mathrm{Proj}_1$) holds constructively if and only if $M$ has a right zero $e \in M$ (\texttt{monoid\_ proj1\_ iff\_ rightZero}, cf.~\cite{HemelaerRogers21}). Requiring open-subtopos stability banishes raw $\mathrm{Proj}_1$-dogma via the $2$-object \textbf{Freyd cover} (scone) category $\mathcal{C} = (\mathbf{B}(\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}))_\bot$ (\texttt{BZ2BotObj} with objects $\{\mathrm{bot}, \mathrm{star}\}$, $\mathrm{End}(\mathrm{bot}) = \{\mathrm{id}\}$, $\mathrm{End}(\mathrm{star}) = \mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}$, a unique morphism $\mathrm{bot} \to \mathrm{star}$, and $\mathrm{Hom}(\mathrm{star}, \mathrm{bot}) = \emptyset$): $\mathbf{Set}^{\mathcal{C}}$ satisfies $\mathrm{Proj}_1$ ($\Gamma(F) \simeq F(\mathrm{bot})$) and fails $\mathrm{IAC}$ ($\emptyset < U_{\mathrm{star}} < 1$), whereas its open subtopos $\mathbf{Set}^{\mathcal{C}}/U_{\mathrm{star}} \simeq \mathbf{Set}^{\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}}$ satisfies $\mathrm{IAC} \wedge \neg\mathrm{AC}$ and refutes $\mathrm{Proj}_1$ (\texttt{freydCover\_ dogma\_ banishment}), tightening $\mathrm{Proj}_1$ to hereditary subterminal projectivity $\mathrm{Proj}_{\mathrm{sub}} = \mathrm{SS}$ (Freyd--{\v{S}}{\v{c}}edrov's~\cite{FreydScedrov90} \emph{Supports Split}), separated constructively from $\mathrm{Proj}_1$ on the $4$-element diamond poset $D_4$ (\texttt{diamond\_ proj1\_ not\_ projSub}).
  \item \textbf{Finite Pareto Schism, $\mathrm{Choice}$-Free Finite Separations, and Slice Count:} Evaluated across the four-topos test suite $\{\mathbf{Set}, \mathbf{Set}^{\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}}, \mathbf{Set}^{\mathcal{M}_1}, \mathbf{Set}^{V^{\mathrm{op}}}\}$ with hypothesis $H = \mathrm{Ext}(\{\mathrm{IAC}\})$ and conclusion $C = \mathbf{Mod}(\mathrm{AC}) = \{\mathbf{Set}\}$ (distinct from $\mathrm{Ext}(\{\mathrm{Proj}_{\mathrm{sub}}\})$, \texttt{choiceConcl\_ ne\_ extProj}, since $\mathbf{Set}^{\mathcal{M}_1} \models \mathrm{Proj}_{\mathrm{sub}} \wedge \neg\mathrm{IAC}$, \texttt{not\_ catPresheafIAC\_ m1Cat}, leaving $T = \{\mathbf{Set}, \mathbf{Set}^{\mathcal{M}_1}, \mathbf{Set}^{V^{\mathrm{op}}}\}$), the Pareto frontier $\mathrm{Res}_{\mathcal{P}}^{\min}(H \Rightarrow C)$ splits into the incomparable pair of extents $\mathrm{Ext}(\{\mathrm{Proj}_{\mathrm{sub}}\})$ and $\mathrm{Ext}(\{\mathrm{Localic}\})$ (\texttt{pareto\_ incomparable\_ projSub\_ localic}, where all finite-witness negative and combinatorial separations are Choice-free while ambient \texttt{Classical.choice} enters only for positive \texttt{Type}-presheaf properties such as $\mathbf{Set} \models \mathrm{AC}$, $\mathbf{Set}^{\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}} \models \mathrm{IAC}$, and \texttt{catPresheafProjSub\_ m1Cat}):
  \begin{itemize}
    \item the $2$-element absorbing base monoid $\mathcal{M}_1 = (\{e_1, 1_1\}, \wedge)$ of Chapter~\ref{ch:higher-crm} (\texttt{M1Monoid} $\simeq$ \texttt{AbsorbingMonoidCarrier 1}) satisfies $\mathrm{Proj}_{\mathrm{sub}}$ via $u \mapsto e_1 \cdot x_0$ while failing localicity since $e_1 \neq 1_1$ (\texttt{m1\_ projSub\_ and\_ not\_ localic}); and
    \item the $3$-element V-poset $V = \{a > c < b\}$ is localic by Yoneda generation yet fails $\mathrm{Proj}_1$ (hence fails $\mathrm{Proj}_{\mathrm{sub}}$) on $\mathbf{y}(a) \amalg \mathbf{y}(b)$ and fails $\mathrm{IAC}$ (\texttt{vPoset\_ localic\_ not\_ proj1}, \texttt{not\_ catPresheafIAC\_ vPosetOpCat}).
  \end{itemize}
  Moreover, over any finite idempotent $\mathcal{M}_1$-set $(X, \rho)$ with $k$ fixed points and $m$ non-fixed points, the slice topos $\mathbf{Set}^{\mathcal{M}_1}/(X, \rho)$ admits exactly $3^k 2^m$ Lawvere--Tierney topologies, splitting cleanly into $2^{k+m}$ localic slices and $(3^k - 2^k) 2^m$ non-localic (and non-Boolean) slices (\texttt{M1SliceLTT. m1\_ slice\_ lt\_ count}).
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
For~(i), $\mathbf{1} \in \Sigma$ gives $\Box_\Sigma(T) \subseteq T$; closure of $\Sigma$ under composition gives $\sigma(\tau(X)) \in T \implies \tau(X) \in \Box_\Sigma(T)$ ($\Sigma$-invariance); and $\sigma(\top) = \top$, $\sigma(X \sqcap Y) = \sigma(X) \sqcap \sigma(Y)$ transport product closure from $T$ to $\Box_\Sigma(T)$, while every $\Sigma$-invariant subclass $\mathcal{U} \subseteq T$ lies inside $\Box_\Sigma(T)$. For~(ii), the right-zero characterization ($\mathbf{Set}^M \models \mathrm{Proj}_1 \iff \exists e \in M,\, \forall m,\, me = e$) is immediate by testing the free cyclic $M$-set $M$ at $1 \in M$ and conversely mapping any inhabited $X$ to $e \cdot x_0$. On the $2$-object Freyd cover $\mathcal{C} = (\mathbf{B}(\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}))_\bot$, every global element of an inhabited presheaf $F : \mathcal{C} \to \mathbf{Set}$ is determined uniquely by $F(\mathrm{bot})$ ($\Gamma(F) \simeq F(\mathrm{bot})$, giving $\mathrm{Proj}_1$ Choice-freely), and Diaconescu pushout against the open subterminal $U_{\mathrm{star}}$ refutes $\mathrm{IAC}$ in $\mathbf{Set}^{\mathcal{C}}$, while restriction to the open complement $\mathbf{Set}^{\mathcal{C}}/U_{\mathrm{star}} \simeq \mathbf{Set}^{\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}}$ kills global sections of the free $\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}$-torsor (yielding $\mathrm{IAC} \wedge \neg\mathrm{AC} \wedge \neg\mathrm{Proj}_1$); on the diamond $D_4 = \{\bot < l, r < \top\}$, the initial/terminal structure forces $\mathrm{Proj}_1$, whereas over the open subterminal $U = \{\bot, l, r\}$ the presheaf $\mathbf{y}(l) \amalg \mathbf{y}(r)$ splits locally without gluing at $\bot$. For~(iii), on $\mathcal{M}_1$ every surjection onto an inhabited subterminal $1$ picks $e_1 \cdot x_0$ constructively while non-triviality of $\mathcal{M}_1$ blocks subterminal generation (Choice-freely), and its V-maker cover refutes $\mathrm{IAC}$ Choice-freely; on $V$, Yoneda principal sieves $\downarrow p$ generate $\mathbf{Set}^{V^{\mathrm{op}}}$ Choice-freely while compatibility on $a$ and $b$ diverges at $c$; and in $\mathbf{Set}^{\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}}$ all morphisms are invertible, validating $\mathrm{IAC}$ via \texttt{Classical.choice} on \texttt{Type} while refuting $\mathrm{AC}$ Choice-freely. Thus neither $\mathrm{Proj}_{\mathrm{sub}}$ nor $\mathrm{Localic}$ lies inside the other, yet $\mathrm{Ext}(\{\mathrm{IAC}, \mathrm{Localic}\}) = \mathrm{Ext}(\{\mathrm{IAC}, \mathrm{Proj}_{\mathrm{sub}}\}) = \{\mathbf{Set}\}$ inside the four-topos suite. Finally, decomposing fiberwise modal closures $j_x : \Omega_{\mathcal{M}_1} \to \Omega_{\mathcal{M}_1}$ on $\Omega_{\mathcal{M}_1} = \{\emptyset < \{e_1\} < 1\}$ under $e_1$-equivariance gives $3$ maps on each fixed point ($\mathrm{id}, j_{\mathrm{loc}}, \top$, with two localic choices killing $\mathrm{id}$) and $2$ extensions on each non-fixed point once $j_{\rho(x)}(\emptyset)$ is fixed, yielding $3^k 2^m = 2^{k+m} + (3^k - 2^k)2^m$.
\end{proof}

\section{Paradigmatic Case Study: The Choice-Geometry Residuum Atlas}

The archetypal testing ground for Axis~I of Categorical Reverse Mathematics is the \emph{Choice-Geometry Residuum Atlas} (developed in full in Chapter~\ref{ch:choice-atlas}): given a weak form of choice $A$, what is the weakest structural condition $\mathcal{R}(\mathcal{E})$ on a topos $\mathcal{E}$ such that $A + \mathcal{R}(\mathcal{E}) \iff \mathrm{AC}$?

In any elementary topos $\mathcal{E}$, the \emph{External Axiom of Choice} ($\mathrm{AC}$) states that every epimorphism $e: X \twoheadrightarrow Y$ admits a global section $s: Y \to X$, whereas the \emph{Internal Axiom of Choice} ($\mathrm{IAC}$) asserts that dependent product functors $\Pi_f : \mathcal{E}/X \to \mathcal{E}/Y$ (equivalently exponentials $(-)^Z$) preserve epimorphisms~\cite{Johnstone02}, and \emph{Local Choice} splits epimorphisms locally along covering sieves in every Grothendieck topos $\mathbf{Sh}(\mathbb{C}, J)$. Evaluating $(A \Rightarrow_{\mathbf{Topos}} \mathrm{AC})$ isolates five benchmark transitions:

\begin{enumerate}
  \item \textbf{Trivial Base ($A = \top \Rightarrow \mathrm{AC}$):} By Diaconescu's theorem~\cite{Diaconescu75}, $\mathrm{IAC}$ forces Booleanness ($\Omega \simeq 1+1$), yet Freyd's two-valued Boolean topos $\mathcal{F} = \mathbf{Sh}_{\neg\neg}(\mathbb{A})$ with $\mathrm{NNO}$ still refutes $\mathrm{AC}$~\cite{Freyd72}. Over $\mathbf{Set}_{\mathrm{AC}}$, the Barr--Diaconescu--Freyd theorem~\cite{Barr74,Diaconescu75,Freyd72} (\cite[\S D4.5]{Johnstone02}) identifies $\mathcal{E} \models \mathrm{AC} \iff \mathcal{E} \simeq \mathbf{Sh}(B)$, isolating \emph{Booleanness plus localicity} as $(\top \Rightarrow_{\mathbf{GTop}} \mathrm{AC})$.
  \item \textbf{Internal to External Choice ($\mathrm{IAC} \Rightarrow \mathrm{AC}$):} Bridging internal splittings to global sections $\Gamma(X) = \mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{E}}(1_{\mathcal{E}}, X)$ isolates \emph{external projectivity of $1_{\mathcal{E}}$}: $\mathrm{IAC} + (1_{\mathcal{E}} \text{ externally projective}) \iff \mathrm{AC}$ (Theorem~\ref{thm:iac-to-ac}). In two-valued atomic permutation topoi $\mathbf{Cont}(\mathcal{G})$, any proper open subgroup $U \subsetneq \mathcal{G}$ forces $\Gamma(\mathcal{G}/U) = \emptyset$, separating two-valuedness from external projectivity of $1_{\mathcal{E}}$ (Chapter~\ref{ch:realizability-sites}).
  \item \textbf{Enough Projectives ($\mathrm{COSHEP} \Rightarrow \mathrm{AC}$):} Combining projective covers $\mathrm{COSHEP}$ with Blass's~\cite{Blass79} Small Violations of Choice faces both non-projectivity of $1$ in $\mathbf{Set}^{\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}}$ and non-projectivity of unsplit quotients; Theorem~\ref{thm:coshep-svc} establishes $\mathrm{COSHEP} + (\mathrm{SVC}_{\mathrm{ret}} \wedge \text{Booleanness} \wedge 1_{\mathcal{E}} \text{ externally projective}) \iff \mathrm{AC}$.
  \item \textbf{Boolean Prime Ideal Theorem ($\mathrm{IBPI} \Rightarrow \mathrm{IAC}$):} Where Deligne's theorem~\cite[Exp.~VI, Appendice]{Deligne72} equates external $\mathrm{BPI}$ to coherent spatiality and $\mathrm{IBPI}$ yields ultrafilters on internal Boolean algebras~\cite{Johnstone82,Johnstone02}, Conjecture~\ref{conj:bpi-ac} posits Booleanness plus \emph{\'etale clopen-partition refinement} of coherent epimorphic covers over $\mathbf{Set}_{\mathrm{AC}}$.
  \item \textbf{Dependent Choice ($\mathrm{IDC} \Rightarrow \mathrm{IAC}$) and Higher Choice ($\mathrm{AC}_n \Rightarrow \mathrm{AC}_\infty$):} While $\mathbf{Sh}(B) \models \mathrm{AC}$ over $\mathbf{Set}_{\mathrm{AC}}$ regardless of $\mathrm{CCC}$~\cite{Barr74,Johnstone02} (which enters over $\mathcal{S} \models \mathrm{DC}$ in Theorem~\ref{thm:bdf-deformed}), Conjecture~\ref{conj:dc-ac} isolates \emph{\'etale $\omega$-presentability of localic groupoid isotropy}~\cite{Johnstone02,Kihara26} over $\mathbf{Set}_{\mathrm{AC}}$; in $(\infty, 1)$-topoi $\mathcal{X}$~\cite{Lurie09}, bridging $\mathrm{AC}_0$ to $\mathrm{AC}_\infty$ isolates $0$-truncated generation (Chapter~\ref{ch:higher-crm}, Theorem~\ref{thm:global-inf-choice}).
\end{enumerate}

\section{Beyond Choice: The Five-Axis Map and a Panorama of Structural Lattices}

Although the Axiom of Choice (Axis~I) provides the archetypal historical arena for $\mathrm{CRM}$, the residuum operator $\mathrm{Res}(\kappa)$ on $\mathrm{Dom}(\mathrm{Res})$ treats all five foundational axes symmetrically, as summarized in Table~\ref{tab:five-axis-master}.

\begin{center}\emph{[Table omitted in blueprint edition; see Royal Octavo monograph PDF.]}\label{tab:five-axis-master}\end{center}

Beyond Axis~I, the residuum calculus organizes classical and constructive bifurcations across seven major non-$\mathrm{AC}$ structural lattices---proved internally along Axes~IV--V in Chapters~\ref{ch:delta-rings} and~\ref{ch:higher-crm} and synthesized against external literature benchmarks in the Programmatic Panorama of Chapter~\ref{ch:panorama}:
\begin{itemize}
  \item \textbf{Internal Continuum \& Pointfree Spatiality (Axes~II--III, \S\ref{sec:panorama-continuum-spatial}):} Separating $\mathbb{R}_C \rightarrowtail \mathbb{R}_D \rightarrowtail \mathbb{R}_M$, $\varepsilon\text{-}\mathrm{IVT} \Rightarrow \mathrm{IVT}$, and Johnstone's localic Tychonoff / Mulvey--Pelletier Hahn--Banach theorems from spatial point-set counterparts via countable cover refinement, local connectedness, and internal spatiality~\cite{Bishop67,Ishihara06,Johnstone81,Johnstone82,MulveyPelletier91,Deligne72,Johnstone02}.
  \item \textbf{Intermediate Logics \& Homological Exactness (Axes~I--III, \S\ref{sec:panorama-omega-homological}):} Calibrating $\mathrm{WLEM}$ and G\"odel--Dummett linearity via $\Omega$-geometry~\cite{Johnstone82,Johnstone02}, and separating Bourn protomodularity~\cite{Bourn91}, Barr-exactness~\cite[\S\S A1.3--A1.4]{Johnstone02}, semi-abelian Snake Lemma naturality~\cite{JMT02}, and $\mathbf{AB5}$ vs.\ $\mathbf{AB4}^*$ ($\varprojlim^1$).
  \item \textbf{Predicative Pretopoi, $W$-Types, \& Smooth Cohesion (Axes~II--IV, Ch.~\ref{ch:delta-rings} \& \S\S\ref{sec:predicativity-wtypes}, \ref{sec:cohesive-lattice}):} Separating Maietti--Joyal Arithmetic Universes~\cite{Maietti05}, two-sorted Lawvere hyperdoctrines~\cite{Lawvere69} for the classical ``Big Five''~\cite{Friedman75,Simpson09} (below $\mathsf{CZF}/\mathsf{KP}$~\cite{Aczel78} and $\mathsf{Mac}$~\cite{Cole73}; Core Conjecture~5), Kock--Lawvere microlinearity, local tinyness of $D$, and global Lawvere--Schreiber cohesion ($\CohesiveQuad$)~\cite{Kock06,MoerdijkReyes91,Diaconescu75,Lawvere07,Schreiber13}, and Joyal $\delta$-ring $2$-torsion lift torsors ($R_2$ vs.\ \texttt{DualInt})~\cite{Joyal85}.
  \item \textbf{Directed $(\infty, \infty)$-Localizations \& $\mathrm{CatSp}$ (Axis~V, Ch.~\ref{ch:higher-crm} \& \S\ref{subsec:excision-catsp}):} Navigating Ozornova--Rovelli--Walde and Gepner--Heine's four-tier ladder $\mathrm{Cat}^L_{(\infty,\infty)} \subsetneq \mathrm{Cat}^\omega_{(\infty,\infty)} \subsetneq \mathrm{Cat}^{\mathrm{coind}}_{(\infty,\infty)} \subsetneq \mathrm{Cat}^R_{(\infty,\infty)}$~\cite{ORW26,GepnerHeine26}, step-product phantoms ($\mathcal{Q}$), and $\pi_{\le 0}$ group-completion birthing $\pi_*^s(\mathbb{S}_{\mathrm{top}})$~\cite{Heine26,Geiss26,BarrattPriddy72,Quillen71}.
\end{itemize}

\section{Pure Axiom-of-Infinity Residua in $\mathbf{FinSet}$}\label{sec:finset-infinity}

\begin{theorem}[Pure Axiom-of-Infinity Residua in $\mathbf{FinSet}$]\label{thm:finset-infinity-residua}%
\lean{HomotopyCRM.isEmpty_hom_of_fintype_to_succ_lasso, HomotopyCRM.profiniteLassoToZHat_surjective, HomotopyCRM.zHatToProfiniteLasso_neg_one_not_mem_range_nat, HomotopyCRM.dedekindResiduum_eq_empty_of_fintype, HomotopyCRM.nat_succ_mem_dedekindResiduum, HomotopyCRM.nominalDedekindResiduum_eq_empty, HomotopyCRM.not_hasFiniteSupportMap_nat_succ, HomotopyCRM.subsingleton_tailCokernel_of_fintype, HomotopyCRM.nontrivial_tailCokernel_nat, HomotopyCRM.chapter1_pure_infinity_residua_summary}%
\leanok%
\uses{def:comparison-triple,def:residuum}%
Inside $\mathbf{FinSet}$, although $\mathrm{LEM}$ and External Choice ($\mathrm{AC}$) hold unconditionally, withholding a Natural Numbers Object ($\mathrm{NNO}$) along Axis~III precipitates three non-trivial structural residuums: the profinite lasso phantom $\widehat{\mathbb{N}}_{\mathrm{lasso}} \twoheadrightarrow \widehat{\mathbb{Z}}$, the Dedekind/Pigeonhole versus nominal $\mathbb{A}$ finite-support crossing point, and the biproduct-tail cokernel $\prod_{\mathbb{N}} A / \bigoplus_{\mathbb{N}} A \neq 0$.
\end{theorem}

Before relativizing Choice in Chapter~\ref{ch:choice-atlas}, the comparison-square framework of Definitions~\ref{def:comparison-triple}--\ref{def:residuum} reveals a phenomenon invisible inside choice-centric topos theory: \emph{pure structural residuums can crystallize even when $\mathrm{LEM}$ and External Choice ($\mathrm{AC}$) hold $100\%$}. Indeed, Proposition~\ref{prop:tri-modal-factorization} explains why two opposite Mode~(ii) completion polarities diverge along Axis~III: the pro-initial lasso phantom $\widehat{\mathbb{N}}_{\mathrm{lasso}}$ vanishes precisely upon adjoining a Natural Numbers Object ($\mathrm{NNO}$), whereas the Dedekind gap $\mathrm{Res}_{\mathrm{Ded}}(X)$ and biproduct-tail cokernel $\prod_{\mathbb{N}} A / \bigoplus_{\mathbb{N}} A$ vanish precisely under $\neg\mathsf{Inf}$ in $\mathbf{FinSet}$.

Consider the elementary topos $\mathcal{E} = \mathbf{FinSet} \in \mathbf{Topos}$ of finite sets and maps (internally modeling hereditarily finite sets $V_\omega$, i.e., $\mathsf{ZFC} - \mathsf{Inf} + \neg\mathsf{Inf}$). Inside $\mathbf{FinSet}$, the subobject classifier is strictly Boolean ($\Omega \simeq 1+1$), $1$ is externally projective, and every epimorphism $e : X \twoheadrightarrow Y$ admits a global section: \emph{every Choice residuum vanishes identically}. Yet because $\mathbf{FinSet}$ lacks a Natural Numbers Object ($\mathrm{NNO}$), comparing finitary constructions against infinitary doctrines along Axis~III precipitates three distinct, sharp Axiom-of-Infinity residuums---each machine-checked in \texttt{HomotopyCRM/ InfinityResiduum.lean} (949 lines, 20 \texttt{\#print axioms} checks under the 4-Layer Definition Check Gate):

\begin{enumerate}

  \item \textbf{The Profinite Lasso Initial-Algebra Phantom ($\widehat{\mathbb{N}}_{\mathrm{lasso}} \twoheadrightarrow \widehat{\mathbb{Z}}$).} Consider the polynomial successor endofunctor $S(X) = 1 \amalg X$. Inside any doctrine possessing an $\mathrm{NNO}$ ($\mathbf{Pretopos}_{\mathrm{NNO}}$), the initial algebra $\mu S = \mathbb{N}$ exists. Inside $\mathbf{FinSet}$, every $S$-algebra $(X, x_0 : 1 \to X, s : X \to X)$ generated by $x_0$ must eventually collide, forming a finite \emph{lasso} $L_{p,q}$ ($p \ge 0$ stem length, $q \ge 1$ cycle period, formalized as \texttt{lassoDynSys}) satisfying $s^{p+q}(x_0) = s^p(x_0)$ and $|L_{p,q}| = p + q$; no finite carrier $A$ admits a pointed dynamical homomorphism into $L_{|A|, 1}$ (\texttt{isEmpty\_ hom\_ of\_ fintype\_ to\_ succ\_ lasso}). Passing to the profinite completion $\mathrm{Pro}(\mathbf{FinSet})$ along the coherent surjective transition system $L_{p', q'} \twoheadrightarrow L_{p, q}$ for $p \le p'$ and $q \mid q'$ (\texttt{lassoTransition}) precipitates the initial-algebra failure residuum as the profinite lasso inverse limit
  \[
    \mathrm{Res}_{\mathrm{init}}(S; \mathbf{FinSet}) \;=\; \widehat{\mathbb{N}}_{\mathrm{lasso}} \;:=\; \varprojlim_{(p,q)} L_{p,q} \;\notin\; \mathbf{FinSet},
  \]
  whose periodic quotient ($p = 0$) surjects onto the ring of \textbf{profinite integers} $\widehat{\mathbb{Z}} = \varprojlim_{q \ge 1} \mathbb{Z}/q\mathbb{Z}$ (\texttt{profiniteLassoToZHat\_ surjective}), with non-natural element $-1 \in \widehat{\mathbb{Z}}$ separated from $\mathbb{N}$ in \texttt{zHatToProfiniteLasso\_ neg\_ one\_ not\_ mem\_ range\_ nat}. Thus $\widehat{\mathbb{N}}_{\mathrm{lasso}}$ crystallizes externally in $\mathrm{Pro}(\mathbf{FinSet})$ as the exact obstruction between $\mathbf{FinSet}$ and an $\mathrm{NNO}$-bearing doctrine.

  \item \textbf{The Dedekind / Pigeonhole Residuum and the Nominal Crossing Point.} For any object $X$ in a Cartesian category, compare the subobject of automorphisms to monomorphic endomorphisms via $\kappa_{\mathrm{Ded}, X} : \mathrm{Iso}(X, X) \hookrightarrow \mathrm{Mono}(X, X)$, defining the Dedekind residuum
  \[
    \mathrm{Res}_{\mathrm{Ded}}(X) \;:=\; \mathrm{Mono}(X, X) \setminus \mathrm{Iso}(X, X).
  \]
  By the Pigeonhole Principle, $\mathrm{Res}_{\mathrm{Ded}}(X) = \emptyset$ unconditionally for every $X \in \mathbf{FinSet}$ (\texttt{dedekindResiduum\_ eq\_ empty\_ of\_ fintype}), whereas $\mathrm{Res}_{\mathrm{Ded}}(\mathbb{N}) \neq \emptyset$ via $n \mapsto n + 1$ (\texttt{nat\_ succ\_ mem\_ dedekindResiduum}) whenever an $\mathrm{NNO}$ is present. Remarkably, inside Schanuel's nominal topos $\mathbf{Nom} = \mathbf{Cont}(\mathrm{Sym}(\mathbb{N}))$ (Chapter~\ref{ch:realizability-sites}), the constant sheaf $\Delta(\mathbb{N})$ exists---so the Axiom of Infinity and an $\mathrm{NNO}$ hold---yet on the Kuratowski-infinite object of atoms $\mathbb{A}$, finite support forces every injective equivariant map $f : \mathbb{A} \to \mathbb{A}$ to be surjective (any omitted atom outside $\mathrm{supp}(f)$ can be transposed into the range, blocking \texttt{Nat.succ} via \texttt{not\_ hasFiniteSupportMap\_ nat\_ succ}), yielding
  \[
    \mathrm{Mono}_{\mathbf{Nom}}(\mathbb{A}, \mathbb{A}) \;=\; \mathrm{Iso}_{\mathbf{Nom}}(\mathbb{A}, \mathbb{A}), \qquad \mathrm{Res}_{\mathrm{Ded}}(\mathbb{A}) \;=\; \emptyset
  \]
  (\texttt{nominalDedekindResiduum\_ eq\_ empty}). Thus $\mathbb{A} \in \mathbf{Nom}$ exhibits a pure crossing point between Axis~III and Axis~I, cleanly isolating $\mathsf{Inf} \wedge \neg\mathsf{AC}_\omega$ (an internally Kuratowski-infinite object that remains Dedekind-finite).

  \item \textbf{The Biproduct-Tail Cokernel ($\prod_{\mathbb{N}} A / \bigoplus_{\mathbb{N}} A$).} For any additive or abelian coefficient object $A \neq 0$ and index object $I$, consider the canonical inclusion of finitely supported sums into arbitrary products:
  \[
    \kappa_{I, A} : \bigoplus_{i \in I} A \;\hookrightarrow\; \prod_{i \in I} A.
  \]
  For every finite index set $I \in \mathbf{FinSet}$ ($\neg\mathsf{Inf}$), $\kappa_{I, A}$ is an isomorphism and \texttt{TailCokernel I A} is subsingleton (\texttt{subsingleton\_ tailCokernel\_ of\_ fintype}). Adjoining $I = \mathbb{N}$ via an $\mathrm{NNO}$ breaks the isomorphism and precipitates the non-trivial asymptotic \textbf{tail cokernel} (\texttt{nontrivial\_ tailCokernel\_ nat} and \texttt{chapter1\_ pure\_ infinity\_ residua\_ summary}):
  \[
    \mathrm{Res}_{\mathrm{tail}}(\mathbb{N}, A) \;:=\; \prod_{n \in \mathbb{N}} A \,\Big/\, \bigoplus_{n \in \mathbb{N}} A \;\neq\; 0.
  \]
  This single tail quotient foreshadows two of the monograph's central constructions across Axes~I, III, and~V: in Chapter~\ref{ch:base-fiber} (\S\ref{sec:exotic-r4-e0}, specializing to $A = \mathbb{F}_2$), it gives the profinite hyperfinite Connes quotient $\mathcal{Q}_{E_0} = 2^{\mathbb{N}}/E_0 = \prod_{\mathbb{N}} \mathbb{F}_2 / \bigoplus_{\mathbb{N}} \mathbb{F}_2$ obstructing Borel classification of radial exotic $\mathbb{R}^4$'s~\cite{GompfPanagiotopoulos26}; and in Chapter~\ref{ch:higher-crm} (Theorem~\ref{thm:step-product-counterexample}), replacing additive direct sums by finite-prefix sequences $e^{(m)} = (1_1, \dots, 1_m, 0_{m+1}, \dots)$ inside $\mathcal{Q} \subset \prod_{k \ge 1} \mathcal{A}_k$ generates the pure Postnikov phantom $X_\bullet \in RL(\mathcal{Q}) \setminus \mathcal{Q}$.

\end{enumerate}

\section{Relativization and the Base-Fiber Conservation Principle}\label{sec:base-fiber}

Unlike classical reverse mathematics, which is anchored to a single immutable metatheory, Categorical Reverse Mathematics is doubly relative across comparison squares of cloven doctrinal fibrations and adjunctions $F \dashv U : \mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{E}} \rightleftarrows \mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{S}}$: along a geometric morphism $\gamma : \mathcal{E} \to \mathcal{S}$, the Beck--Chevalley mate square comparing internal slice splitting against direct-image pushforward $\gamma_* : \mathcal{E} \to \mathcal{S}$ couples deformations of the ambient universe $\mathcal{E}$ itself (Axis~1) with variations of the metamathematical base universe $\mathcal{S}$ (Axis~2).

Along Axis~1, weakening the Lawvere--Tierney axioms of $\mathcal{E}$ tests the robustness of categorical residuums. Dropping $\Omega$ moves us from elementary topoi to predicative pretopoi, where Diaconescu's theorem ($\mathrm{IAC} \implies \mathrm{LEM}$) still holds~\cite{Diaconescu75,Maietti05} (\cite[\S D4.5]{Johnstone02}), demonstrating that the choice-to-Booleanness bridge does not depend on impredicative comprehension. Dropping exponentials $Y^X$ moves us to coherent and regular categories, forcing local Cartesian closure into the residuum of $\mathrm{IAC}$, while dropping the Natural Numbers Object $\mathrm{NNO}$ isolates the finitary residuums of Section~\ref{sec:finset-infinity} in $\mathbf{FinSet}$.

Along Axis~2, varying the base topos $\mathcal{S}$ exposes the distinction between \emph{absolute} (Beck--Chevalley mate-invariant under pullback along $\gamma^*$) and \emph{relative} (direct-image $\gamma_*$-sensitive) residuums. Purely internal equivalences, such as $\mathrm{IAC} \implies \Omega_{\mathcal{E}} \simeq 1+1$, are preserved unconditionally by $\gamma^*$. By contrast, external equivalences---such as the Barr--Diaconescu--Freyd theorem ($\mathcal{E} \models \mathrm{AC} \iff \mathcal{E} \simeq \mathbf{Sh}_{\mathcal{S}}(B)$)~\cite{Barr74,Diaconescu75,Freyd72} or Deligne's spatiality theorem for coherent topoi~\cite{Deligne72}---transpose via the Beck--Chevalley mate $\gamma^* \dashv \gamma_*$ to depend on $\mathrm{AC}$ or $\mathrm{BPI}$ holding in the base topos $\mathcal{S}$.

When $\mathrm{AC}$ or $\mathrm{BPI}$ is weakened in the base $\mathcal{S}$, Beck--Chevalley mate transposition does not allow total strength to disappear; instead, the Universal Doctrinal Square Defect obeys the \emph{Base-Fiber Conservation Principle}:

\begin{principle}[Conservation of Logical Strength Across Geometric Morphisms]\label{prin:conservation}%
\uses{def:comparison-triple,def:residuum}%

For any external mathematical theorem $B$ evaluated in a relative topos $\gamma: \mathcal{E} \to \mathcal{S}$ over a weaker principle $A$, the total logical and structural strength required to validate $B$ is conserved across the base $\mathcal{S}$ and the fiber $\mathcal{E}$. Any choice, compactness, or classical logic axiom removed from the metamathematical base $\mathcal{S}$ reappears as a strictly stronger topological, atomic, or cohomological rigidity condition inside the relative residuum $\mathcal{R}(\mathcal{E}/\mathcal{S})$.

\end{principle}

For example, if $\mathrm{BPI}$ is dropped in the base $\mathcal{S}$, coherent $\mathcal{S}$-topoi lose spatiality, and the residuum required to bridge pointfree localic theorems to spatial theorems must upgrade from \emph{finitary coherence} (every cover has a finite subcover) to \emph{supercompactness} (every cover refines to a single element), bypassing the need for base ultrafilters~\cite{Deligne72} (\cite[Ch.~II, \S\S 2--4]{Johnstone82}, \cite[\S D3.3]{Johnstone02}). Through this conservation principle (developed quantitatively in Chapter~\ref{ch:base-fiber}), Categorical Reverse Mathematics unifies the internal logic of topoi, the geometry of sites, and the metamathematics of set theory into a single structural framework.

\chapter{The Choice-Geometry Residuum Atlas and Cohomological Obstruction Theory}

\label{ch:choice-atlas}

\section{The Anatomy of Choice in Topos Theory}

Interpreting Axis~I of the doctrinal comparison framework (Definitions~\ref{def:comparison-triple}--\ref{def:residuum}) across $\mathcal{D}_0 = \mathbf{Topos} \supseteq \mathbf{Topos}_{\mathrm{AC}} = \mathcal{D}_1$ compares global epimorphism sections against local covers. In classical set theory over $\mathrm{ZF}$, the Axiom of Choice ($\mathrm{AC}$) and its weaker relatives---such as Dependent Choice ($\mathrm{DC}$), Countable Choice ($\mathrm{CC}$), the Presentation Axiom ($\mathrm{COSHEP}$), $\mathrm{WISC}$, and the Boolean Prime Ideal Theorem ($\mathrm{BPI}$)---are formulated as existential assertions about subsets and functions within a single cumulative hierarchy. When we pass to an arbitrary elementary or Grothendieck topos $\mathcal{E}$, these principles bifurcate into distinct internal and external structural properties governing how dependent products, exponentials, and global section functors behave.

To construct the \emph{Choice-Geometry Residuum Atlas}, we establish the precise categorical definitions of the choice hierarchy inside an ambient universe $\mathcal{E}$:

\begin{itemize}

  \item \textbf{External Axiom of Choice ($\mathrm{AC}$):} Every epimorphism $e: X \twoheadrightarrow Y$ in $\mathcal{E}$ admits a global section $s: Y \to X$ satisfying $e \circ s = \mathrm{id}_Y$, meaning every object $Y \in \mathcal{E}$ is externally projective.

  \item \textbf{Internal Axiom of Choice ($\mathrm{IAC}$):} For every morphism $f: X \to Y$, the dependent product functor $\Pi_f: \mathcal{E}/X \to \mathcal{E}/Y$ (right adjoint to pullback $f^*$) preserves epimorphisms~\cite{Johnstone02}---equivalently, for every epimorphism $e: X \twoheadrightarrow Y$ and object $Z \in \mathcal{E}$, the exponential map $e^Z: X^Z \to Y^Z$ is an epimorphism (every object is internally projective).

  \item \textbf{Enough Projectives ($\mathrm{COSHEP}$):} Every object $X \in \mathcal{E}$ can be covered by an epimorphism $P \twoheadrightarrow X$ from an externally projective object $P$, corresponding to the categorical Presentation Axiom.

  \item \textbf{Weakly Initial Set of Covers ($\mathrm{WISC}$):} For every object $X \in \mathcal{E}$ there exists a small (set-indexed) family of epimorphisms $\{q_i : Y_i \twoheadrightarrow X\}_{i \in I}$ through at least one of which every epimorphism $E \twoheadrightarrow X$ factors (van den Berg--Moerdijk--Streicher, weakening $\mathrm{COSHEP}$ by removing projectivity of $Y_i$).

  \item \textbf{Small Violations of Choice ($\mathrm{SVC}$ and $\mathrm{SVC}_{\mathrm{ret}}$):} Andreas Blass's~\cite{Blass79} $\mathrm{SVC}$ posits a single parameterizing object $S \in \mathcal{E}$ such that every object $X \in \mathcal{E}$ is a subquotient of $C \times S$ for a projective choice object $C$; its retract refinement $\mathrm{SVC}_{\mathrm{ret}}$ requires a parameter $S$ with projective cover $q : P \twoheadrightarrow S$ such that every $X \in \mathcal{E}$ is a retract of a subobject $U \rightarrowtail C \times P$ with $C \times P$ projective (equivalently, the presentation quotient over $C \times P$ splits).

  \item \textbf{Sequential Choice ($\mathrm{DC}$, $\mathrm{IDC}$, and $\mathrm{CC}$):} In a topos with a Natural Numbers Object ($\mathrm{NNO}$), external $\mathrm{DC}$ asserts that every countable inverse tower of epimorphisms $\cdots \twoheadrightarrow X_2 \twoheadrightarrow X_1 \twoheadrightarrow X_0$ admits a global limit projection splitting, while Internal Dependent Choice ($\mathrm{IDC}$) asserts that the inverse limit projection $\varprojlim_{n \in \omega} X_n \twoheadrightarrow X_0$ remains an epimorphism in every slice $\mathcal{E}/E$.

  \item \textbf{Boolean Prime Ideal Theorem ($\mathrm{BPI}$ and $\mathrm{IBPI}$):} Externally, every coherent topos has enough points (Deligne's theorem~\cite{Deligne72}); internally ($\mathrm{IBPI}$), every non-trivial internal Boolean algebra $B \in \mathbf{BA}(\mathcal{E})$ admits an ultrafilter (equivalently, internal Stone/coherent locales are compact~\cite{Johnstone82,Johnstone02}).

  \item \textbf{Universal Local Choice:} In any Grothendieck sheaf topos $\mathcal{E} \simeq \mathbf{Sh}(\mathbb{C}, J)$, every epimorphism $e: X \twoheadrightarrow Y$ splits locally along a covering sieve of the site $(\mathbb{C}, J)$.

\end{itemize}

A foundational structural asymmetry separates external $\mathrm{AC}$ ($\mathrm{EAC}$) from internal choice ($\mathrm{IAC}$). While external $\mathrm{AC}$ implies $\mathrm{IAC}$ in every elementary topos, $\mathrm{IAC}$ is a purely internal local-Cartesian property: $\mathcal{E} \models \mathrm{IAC}$ if and only if for every morphism $f: X \to Y$, the \emph{dependent product functor} $\Pi_f: \mathcal{E}/X \to \mathcal{E}/Y$ (the right adjoint to pullback $f^*$) preserves epimorphisms~\cite{Johnstone02} (Elephant \S D4.5), or equivalently every exponential $(-)^Z : \mathcal{E} \to \mathcal{E}$ preserves epimorphisms. (Notice that the pullback functor $f^*$ itself preserves epimorphisms unconditionally in \emph{every} regular category and elementary topos because colimits are universal.) Because $\Pi_f$ commutes with slice restriction, $\mathrm{IAC}$ is inherited by all slice topoi $\mathcal{E}/E$. External $\mathrm{AC}$, by contrast, tests whether internal splittings survive the global sections functor $\Gamma = \mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{E}}(1_{\mathcal{E}}, -): \mathcal{E} \to \mathbf{Set}$---which depends on external projectivity of $1_{\mathcal{E}}$, a property strictly stronger than two-valuedness ($\mathrm{Sub}_{\mathcal{E}}(1_{\mathcal{E}}) \simeq \{0, 1\}$), as atomic permutation topoi demonstrate.

\section{Diaconescu's Theorem, Freyd's Countermodel, and the Fundamental Residuums}

Before calibrating intermediate choice principles, Categorical Reverse Mathematics must determine the absolute baseline residuums: $(\top \Rightarrow_{\mathbf{Topos}} \mathrm{AC})$ and $(\mathrm{IAC} \Rightarrow_{\mathbf{Topos}} \mathrm{AC})$. The first bridge between choice and internal logic is given by Diaconescu's theorem~\cite{Diaconescu75}.

\begin{theorem}[Diaconescu's Theorem and Predicative Stability]\label{thm:diaconescu}%
\uses{def:comparison-triple,def:residuum}%

In any elementary topos $\mathcal{E}$~\cite{Diaconescu75}---and more generally in any locally Cartesian closed pretopos~\cite[\S D4.5, Thm.~D4.5.1--Cor.~D4.5.2]{Johnstone02} (cf.~\cite{Maietti05})---the Internal Axiom of Choice implies that the internal logic is Boolean ($\mathrm{IAC} \implies \Omega \simeq 1+1$, or internal $\mathrm{LEM}$).

\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}[Proof Sketch]
Given any subobject $U \rightarrowtail X$, one constructs the pushout $X +_U X$ of two copies of $X$ glued along $U$, equipped with the canonical codiagonal epimorphism $e: X + X \twoheadrightarrow X +_U X$. Pulling back or exponentiating $e$ via $\mathrm{IAC}$ produces an internal section whose preimages decompose $X$ into $U \amalg \neg U$, proving that every subobject lattice $\mathrm{Sub}_{\mathcal{E}}(X)$ is complemented. Because this construction requires only finite colimits, pullbacks, and local Cartesian closure rather than impredicative power objects $\Omega^X$, the implication $\mathrm{IAC} \implies \mathrm{LEM}$ is predicative and base-invariant across any base topos $\mathcal{S}$.
\end{proof}

Diaconescu's theorem proves that Booleanness ($\Omega \simeq 1+1$) is an obligatory factor of the residuum $(A \Rightarrow_{\mathbf{Topos}} \mathrm{AC})$ whenever the weak principle $A$ is consistent with non-Boolean intuitionistic logic. However, Booleanness and $\mathrm{IAC}$ do not suffice to split epimorphisms externally.

\begin{theorem}[The Internal-to-External Choice Residuum: $\mathrm{IAC} \Rightarrow \mathrm{AC}$]\label{thm:iac-to-ac}%
\uses{thm:diaconescu}%

In an elementary topos $\mathcal{E}$, the categorical residuum bridging the Internal Axiom of Choice to the External Axiom of Choice is the external projectivity of the terminal object $1_{\mathcal{E}}$:

\[
  \mathrm{IAC} + \bigl(1_{\mathcal{E}} \text{ is externally projective}\bigr) \iff \mathrm{AC}
\]

In particular, whenever $1_{\mathcal{E}}$ is externally projective (and a fortiori if $\mathcal{E}$ is a well-pointed topos), $\mathrm{IAC} \iff \mathrm{AC}$ unconditionally.

\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
If $\mathcal{E} \models \mathrm{AC}$, every object (including $1_{\mathcal{E}}$) is externally projective, and $\mathrm{AC} \implies \mathrm{IAC}$. Conversely, suppose $\mathcal{E} \models \mathrm{IAC}$ and $1_{\mathcal{E}}$ is externally projective. For any epimorphism $e: X \twoheadrightarrow Y$, internal choice ($\mathrm{IAC}$) guarantees that the exponential morphism $e^Y: X^Y \twoheadrightarrow Y^Y$ is an epimorphism. Pulling $e^Y$ back along the name of the identity morphism $\ulcorner \mathrm{id}_Y \urcorner: 1_{\mathcal{E}} \to Y^Y$ yields the internal object of sections $\mathrm{Sect}(e) \subseteq X^Y$ together with a canonical pullback epimorphism $\mathrm{Sect}(e) \twoheadrightarrow 1_{\mathcal{E}}$. Because $1_{\mathcal{E}}$ is externally projective, the epimorphism $\mathrm{Sect}(e) \twoheadrightarrow 1_{\mathcal{E}}$ splits via a global point $1_{\mathcal{E}} \to \mathrm{Sect}(e)$, which Cartesian adjunction transposes into a global section $s: Y \to X$ satisfying $e \circ s = \mathrm{id}_Y$.
\end{proof}

\noindent\textbf{Remark on Subterminal Generation (Localicity).} In any elementary topos satisfying $\mathrm{AC}$, subterminal generation (localicity: subobjects of $1_{\mathcal{E}}$ generate $\mathcal{E}$) holds automatically as a \emph{consequence} of $\mathrm{AC}$ (since supports of slice objects split). Rather than being needed to bridge $\mathrm{IAC}$ to $\mathrm{AC}$, localicity serves as the exact geometric residuum bridging internal Booleanness ($\mathrm{LEM}$) to $\mathrm{AC}$ in Grothendieck topoi over $\mathbf{Set}_{\mathrm{AC}}$ (Theorem~\ref{thm:bdf}).

When we specialize to Grothendieck topoi over $\mathbf{Set}$ (assuming $\mathrm{AC}$ in the base $\mathbf{Set}$), one might ask whether Booleanness together with two-valuedness ($\mathrm{Sub}(1_{\mathcal{E}}) \simeq \{0, 1\}$) or external projectivity of $1_{\mathcal{E}}$ already forces localicity. Crucially, as detailed in Chapter~\ref{ch:realizability-sites} (\S\ref{subsec:kihara-choice-nominal}), two-valuedness must never be conflated with external projectivity of the terminal object $1_{\mathcal{E}}$: in any connected group-action or atomic permutation topos $\mathbf{Cont}(\mathcal{G})$~\cite[Ch.~III, \S 9]{MacLaneMoerdijk92} (such as $\mathbf{Cont}(\mathrm{Aut}(\mathcal{M}))$ for an infinite Fra\"iss\'e structure $\mathcal{M}$ or Schanuel's nominal topos $\mathbf{Nom}$), $1_{\mathcal{E}}$ is strictly two-valued, yet $1_{\mathcal{E}}$ is \emph{never} externally projective whenever a proper open subgroup $U \subsetneq \mathcal{G}$ exists, since the coset epimorphism $\mathcal{G}/U \twoheadrightarrow 1_{\mathcal{E}}$ has zero fixed points ($\Gamma(\mathcal{G}/U) = (\mathcal{G}/U)^{\mathcal{G}} = \emptyset$). Conversely, in sheaves on a non-trivial complete Boolean algebra $\mathbf{Sh}(B)$ over $\mathbf{Set}_{\mathrm{AC}}$, $1$ is externally projective while failing two-valuedness ($\mathrm{Sub}(1) \simeq B$)~\cite{Barr74} (\cite[\S D4.5]{Johnstone02}; \cite[Chs.~VI, IX]{MacLaneMoerdijk92}). Peter Freyd's classic counterexample~\cite{Freyd72} demonstrates that even in a two-valued Boolean Grothendieck topos with an $\mathrm{NNO}$, localicity need not hold.

\begin{theorem}[Freyd's Countermodel and the Barr--Diaconescu--Freyd Residuum]\label{thm:bdf}%
\uses{thm:diaconescu,thm:iac-to-ac}%

There exists a two-valued, Boolean Grothendieck topos $\mathcal{F} = \mathbf{Sh}_{\neg\neg}(\mathbb{A})$ equipped with a Natural Numbers Object ($\mathrm{NNO}$) in which $\mathrm{AC}$ fails~\cite{Freyd72}. Over $\mathbf{Set}_{\mathrm{AC}}$, a Grothendieck topos $\mathcal{E}$ satisfies $\mathrm{AC}$ if and only if $\mathcal{E} \simeq \mathbf{Sh}(B)$ is equivalent to sheaves on a complete Boolean algebra $B$~\cite{Barr74,Diaconescu75,Freyd72} (\cite[\S D4.5, Thms.~D4.5.3--D4.5.7]{Johnstone02}). Consequently, the baseline Grothendieck residuum is:

\[
  \top + \bigl(\text{Booleanness } (\Omega \simeq 1+1) \wedge \text{Localicity}\bigr) \iff \mathrm{AC}
\]

\end{theorem}

In Freyd's topos $\mathcal{F} = \mathbf{Sh}_{\neg\neg}(\mathbb{A})$, the nontrivial automorphism group of the site prevents subterminal objects from generating the category, creating a countable sequence of epimorphisms that cannot be split by global sections~\cite{Freyd72} (\cite[\S D4.5]{Johnstone02}). Thus, in the lattice of Grothendieck topoi, \emph{localicity} is completely orthogonal to Booleanness and two-valuedness. Moreover, by the \emph{Base-Fiber Conservation Principle} (Chapter~\ref{ch:foundations}, Principle~\ref{prin:conservation}), if $\mathrm{AC}$ is dropped in the base topos $\mathcal{S}$, localicity and Booleanness over $\mathcal{S}$ no longer suffice for $\mathcal{E} \models \mathrm{AC}$; the residuum $\mathcal{R}(\mathcal{E}/\mathcal{S})$ must strengthen to require that the underlying locale $B$ is an internally projective atomic Boolean algebra over a choice slice of $\mathcal{S}$.

\section{Residuums of Intermediate Choice Principles ($\mathrm{COSHEP}$, $\mathrm{WISC}$, $\mathrm{IBPI}$, and $\mathrm{IDC}$)}

We now compute and conjecture the categorical residuums $(A \Rightarrow_{\mathbf{Topos}} \mathrm{AC})$ when $A$ is a non-trivial weak choice axiom. Each weak choice axiom absorbs a specific dimension of projective or topological complexity, leaving a complementary geometric invariant as the residuum $\mathcal{R}(\mathcal{E})$.

\subsection{Projective Covers, Small Violations, and $\mathrm{WISC}$ ($\mathrm{COSHEP} \Rightarrow \mathrm{AC}$)}

In constructive and categorical logic, the Presentation Axiom $\mathrm{COSHEP}$ (``the category of sets has enough projectives'') is strictly weaker than $\mathrm{AC}$ because projective covers $P \twoheadrightarrow X$ do not force $X$ itself to be projective. Between bare topos exactness and $\mathrm{COSHEP}$ sits an even weaker structural principle, \textbf{$\mathrm{WISC}$ (Weakly Initial Set of Covers / Axiom of Multiple Choice)}, asserting that every object $X \in \mathcal{E}$ possesses a small family of epimorphisms $\{q_i : Y_i \twoheadrightarrow X\}_{i \in I}$ through at least one of which every epimorphism $E \twoheadrightarrow X$ factors. Whereas $\mathrm{WISC}$ suffices for predicative coinductive and $W$-type semantics without needing projective covers, $\mathrm{COSHEP}$ strengthens $\mathrm{WISC}$ by asking for a single projective cover $P \twoheadrightarrow X$ (as holds in Martin Hyland's Effective Topos $\Eff$~\cite{Hyland82}, \cite[\S\S 3.1--3.2]{vanOosten08}, Chapter~\ref{ch:realizability-sites}, without $\mathrm{LEM}$ or $\mathrm{AC}$).

Dual to $\mathrm{COSHEP}$ is Andreas Blass's principle of Small Violations of Choice ($\mathrm{SVC}$)~\cite{Blass79}, which bounds non-projectivity across the universe by a single parameter object $S$. Two sharp obstructions govern any attempt to bridge $\mathrm{COSHEP}$ to external $\mathrm{AC}$: first, the discrete group topos $\mathbf{Set}^{\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}}$ is Boolean ($\Omega \simeq 1+1$), satisfies $\mathrm{COSHEP}$ (free regular covers $|X| \times \mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z} \twoheadrightarrow X$), and satisfies $\mathrm{SVC}$ ($S = \mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}$), yet fails $\mathrm{AC}$ because $1$ is not externally projective ($\Gamma(\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}) = \emptyset$); second, whereas complemented subobjects and retracts of projectives are always projective in a Boolean topos, arbitrary \emph{quotients} of projectives are not projective in general (the \emph{quotient fallacy}). Incorporating both external projectivity of $1_{\mathcal{E}}$ and retract presentations yields the exact rectified residuum theorem:

\begin{theorem}[The Rectified $\mathrm{COSHEP}$--$\mathrm{SVC}$ Residuum]\label{thm:coshep-svc}%
\uses{thm:diaconescu,thm:iac-to-ac}%

In an elementary topos $\mathcal{E}$, external $\mathrm{AC}$ holds if and only if $\mathcal{E}$ satisfies $\mathrm{COSHEP}$, Booleanness ($\Omega \simeq 1+1$), external projectivity of the terminal object $1_{\mathcal{E}}$, and Blass's retract-bounded Small Violations of Choice ($\mathrm{SVC}_{\mathrm{ret}}$):

\[
  \mathrm{COSHEP} + \bigl(\mathrm{SVC}_{\mathrm{ret}} \wedge \text{Booleanness} \wedge (1_{\mathcal{E}} \text{ is externally projective})\bigr) \iff \mathrm{AC}
\]

where $\mathrm{SVC}_{\mathrm{ret}}$ guarantees a parameter $S$ with projective cover $q : P \twoheadrightarrow S$ such that every object $X \in \mathcal{E}$ is a retract of a subobject $U \rightarrowtail C \times P$ for a choice object $C$ with $C \times P$ externally projective.

\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Under $\mathrm{AC}$, every object (including $1_{\mathcal{E}}$) is externally projective (so $\mathrm{COSHEP}$ holds), Theorem~\ref{thm:diaconescu} yields Booleanness, and $S = P = 1_{\mathcal{E}}$ with $C = X$ witnesses $\mathrm{SVC}_{\mathrm{ret}}$ ($X \cong X \times 1_{\mathcal{E}}$).

Conversely, assume $\mathrm{COSHEP}$, $\mathrm{SVC}_{\mathrm{ret}}$, Booleanness ($\Omega \simeq 1+1$), and external projectivity of $1_{\mathcal{E}}$. We first prove two exact closure lemmas for externally projective objects:
\begin{enumerate}
